# Local backlog

This is the canonical status source for the tasks below. Keep stable IDs, close with a
receipt in Source, and retain closed rows. The workspace derives its common backlog
from [backlog-sources.json](backlog-sources.json). Dated handoffs remain historical evidence.
The order and dates of the iteration of 2026-10-22 are in [its plan](evidence/plans/2026-10-22-next-iteration.md); all its rows are merged.
Every open row by class, and the proposed order to the first organization, are in [the audit of 2026-10-25](evidence/plans/2026-10-25-first-organization.md).
What is left until the North Star, every open row read against its gaps and ordered in waves to the organization slice of 2026-11-21, is in [the cut of 2026-11-07](evidence/plans/2026-11-07-north-star.md).

The reviewed contract questions are resolved by DEC-0017. The permissive schema exception was
the cross-repository licensing question CO-KB-02, closed on 2026-10-19 (the schemas stay under this repository's licence).

What each adopts: **the collector**: `account.class` with the policy's `tenantRef`, `policy.violation` by UTC day emitted after the day ends, `run.root` on usage lines, nothing uploaded before `collectionStart`, policy keys judged at verification time, locks kept after `expiresAt`, decisions as `FAC-SEM-068`, `components[]` and `records` in check-ins, `deviceKeys` `#sig`/`#kem` (P-256 checked by SEC 1 §3.2.2), path-free detail strings, and no `\udxxx` in any settings-form input; **the organization server**: acks that never move over check-in drops, another person's view of a member only as `FAC-SEM-067` allows, and its owner and administrator views stricter still: per person only usage and cost as sums per day or billing period and compliance as a state without a time (OD-1, the organization edition's rule 10), never a per-person presence view, consent records kept its own way, the proven user bound, `collectionStart` stated, key sets signed in the root's algorithm, the root's certification for organizations a vendor hosts; **Project Observatory's node**: the join of usage lines and Fabric's `result` on `run.root`, the check-in `records` the relay protocol (DEC-0047) fills, and the device keys in the platform keystore, or a file where it has none (DEC-0094). DEC-0056 (board run wave 4, P4-04) adds: **the collector and Project Observatory's node** sign every batch, check-in and rotation request with the device's `#sig` key (label `fabric-device`, a fresh nonce, `keyid` the certificate's serial) and list exactly one `#sig` and at most one `#kem` at enrollment; **the organization server**, behind a TLS-terminating proxy, verifies the signature as `verifyDeviceRequest` does, answers its 401 codes, keeps (serial, nonce) until `created + 300` s, and runs `fixtures/vectors/http-signature.json` at its next pin, where the seam reading found 42 of its 44 first vectors agreeing and these differences to close: (a) its anchored pattern answers `signature-missing` when another signature is beside ours; (b) an off-curve P-256 key on record throws (a 500, not a 401); (c) it accepts an `http://` public base URL; (d) it refuses an expired (serial, nonce) pair until its purge runs; (e) no small-order check at enrollment; (f) it upper-cases `@method`; (g) it verifies under the CSR's key, not the `#sig` key. Its vendored `device-rules.ts` needs nothing new: `FAC-SEM-077` lives in `src/device-transport-rules.ts` with `src/http-signature.ts`, which it adds to its PIN.json `sources` only if it vendors that rule. DEC-0063 and DEC-0129: the organization server lists administrators' signing credentials under its `sign.` RP ID in the `org-key-set/1` drafts the root (held by the owner's Fabric) signs, and never serves a signing page; where finance approvers' are pinned is not decided; DEC-0062: organization windows and their leases wait on a decision beyond the member list (`org-authority/1`, DEC-0129). Goal: interoperability DEC-0061: the first organization server's listings carry what a descriptor's `peer` carries (endpoint, custody, residency (ISO 3166-1 alpha-2) and the maximum of the four data classes), and a listing never runs on a subscription. DEC-0067: the receiver lists `protocols` in its enrollment response, sends records in a revision the node reads, answers `schema-unsupported` after the signature checks, and expects `protocols` only from nodes pinned at or after DEC-0067. DEC-0068: the receiver counts only `session.interval` and `usage.line` (one call per `dedupeKey`), never `session.annotation`, and adds no annotation to a cell's population. Vendoring (reported by its session 2026-10-12): copy each schema's `$ref` closure (`schemaClosure`), never a prefix list. | CT-285 | An autonomy profile per place (North Star gap G19): the operator's decision of 2026-11-09 (fabric-workspace session `a616cbcb`; board request `921edc61`, thread `a3a97cef`) makes which acts an agent finishes end to end without a person a setting per place, not a constant. A headless place (the always-on machine, an organization's space or VM, its cloud) defaults to every act finished autonomously with every permission granted, watched by agents, its checks, receipts and rollback at the process level; a person's own computer defaults to the scenario the person or their administrator chooses. The owner, an organization administrator or a project architect changes the profile within their level, and the administrator agent approves on administrators' commands within those levels; the only human gates left are the body's: a sign-in, a one-time code, a consent screen, a typed secret, a payment or top-up, spend above the owner's limit. Today the contract has no profile and no notion of a place's autonomy: a service refuses a `relayed` action without the operator's decision (`operator-decision-required`, AP-2, DEC-0121), a mandate narrows at each hand-over (DEC-0125), and a runner descriptor names how a runner is driven but not what it may finish alone ([runners](specification/runners.md)). Owed: one DEC, designed with CT-250 so a profile change is read at the same levels from a verified membership attestation and never from a request field: the profile's shape (a place kind, `headless` or `person`, the acts it lets finish without a person, the body's gates that no profile removes), where it lives and who signs it (the organization's record or the estate's), how the board dispatcher and every headless assignment read it (Fabric CO-547, ADR-0168), how it stands as the decision for AP-2, the mandate and `approval/1` inside its bounds, its audit record naming the level and the approver; schema, vectors and FAC-SEM codes. Consumers after the DEC: Fabric CO-547 (`fbd00b82`, the dispatcher and headless assignments), the organization product's levels row (who may change the profile and where it is shown). Goal: interoperability Its DEC restates rules proposed on branches (CT-287) that keep a floored act waiting for a person's session: DEC-0156 OW-3's no-person case and its own stop (with OA-4's pointer to OW-3), and DEC-0158 OS-2 (what a run is handed), OS-6 (the production stop and a host's gate, never resubmitted) and OS-7 (a mandate taken only from the request); whichever of each pair lands second makes the texts agree, and the profile never reaches a usage-data switch a person turned off (DEC-0157 UD-4). Found by the blind readings of 2026-11-12 (DEC-0156 round 25, DEC-0158 round 20, DEC-0157 round 22). Also DEC-0159 CF-4 (a client-key caller's choice at an instance in the local and cloud form waits for the operator, a cloud being a headless place; a client key carries no level or profile, which bind only by token) and CF-3 (client keys the operator's alone), found by DEC-0159's round 16 reading. Read also with DEC-0159 CF-3 (proposed on a branch, CT-218): AU-4's key issuance through the vault's doors does not cover a member's client key at a cloud instance in the local and cloud form until the operator answers DEC-0159's vault-delivery question, and a client key carries no level or profile (CF-4); whichever lands second restates it. | in progress: the design is on main, [the autonomy profile design](evidence/plans/2026-11-12-ct285-autonomy-profile.md) (2026-11-12, board request `4a1bf8d6`): a place's profile as a standing `mandate/1` its hub holds and the dispatcher hands to each assignment, set as `change-policy` by the operator or a CT-250 level, bound at T2 by a new token claim `prof`, the headless default covering every effect class with a declared watcher, three operator questions with defaults; the DEC with CT-250's by 2026-11-21. | CT-286 | Agent metrics: every agent is measurable (board request `24b868d3`, thread `b4c7840a`, 2026-11-10; the operator's word of 2026-11-10 relayed verbatim from the requester's session `429e019c`: «все измерения агентов должны быть измеримыми. Мы должны ввести базу измерений, метрика, основных показателей, которые у нас меняются у агентов, на каких выборках ... это должна быть отдельно проработанная задача на уровне фабрика»). The case: a retro of 2026-11-10 found an agent's automatic launch after a proven payment had failed since 2026-08-01 and a daily autonomy record had never been stored while its routine reported success; no metric of the agent showed either. Today the contract has reports per concern (usage DEC-0021/0027/0065, `service-load/1` DEC-0104, `ops.digest.daily@1` DEC-0153, `support-quality-daily/1` DEC-0095/0144/0151, the evals receipt DEC-0050, activity CT-259) but no registry saying what an agent exists to move, on what population, from what baseline to what target, and no record of whether a change moved it. Owed: one DEC with three schemas and their semantic rules: (1) `agent-metrics/1`, the agent's registry, served at an optional `surfaces.metrics` (service.md): each metric `{id, version}` (a definition never changes in place), `family` (outcome, quality, reliability, cost, speed, revenue, safety, adoption), `means`, `unit`, `direction`, a named `population` (`unit` from a closed list and a `filter` a person can check; a metric without one is refused), `window`, `source.kind` (`usage`, `load`, `ops-digest`, `support-quality`, `eval-receipt`, `activity`, `custom`: the existing reports are pointed at, never duplicated), `baseline` with `n`, `target`, `guardrail`, `owner`; the rule that every agent declares at least one outcome, one quality and one reliability metric; (2) `agent-measurement/1`, one closed UTC period of one `custom` metric (`value`, `n`, `approx`, `notMeasured`), counts and shares only, no person's data, published as an events kind `agent.metric_daily`; a metric not measured is `null` and named in `notMeasured`, never `0` (FAC-SEM-126's rule); (3) `agent-change-measure/1`, whether a change moved a metric: `change {kind, ref}`, `pre` and `post` `{window, value, n}`, `method` (paired, unpaired, naive), `verdict` (improved, worse, no_effect_detected, inconclusive; a small `n` is `inconclusive`, never a win), an optional `prediction` written before the change; designed with CT-264 so its `agents-db-case/1` success case is this record with a case kind, not a second form. To decide in the DEC: whether the requester's fourth form `agent-changes/1` (the registry a release appends to) is contract or the agent's own; the minimum `n` per verdict; the producer and reader rows in conformance.md; fixtures, vectors and FAC-SEM codes. Consumers after the DEC (each asked on the board by the requester 2026-11-10): fabric-agents-database (template `metrics.json`, typed case numbers), fabric-agent-adapter (the creating and adapting skills' grill rows, the services kit module), agent-stack (`agent-evals` online registry), fabric-dashboards (a Metrics tab), project-observatory (stores measurements with an agent reference, a finding for an agent with no outcome metric or a stale one), fabric-workspace (template rule, `knowledge/system/measure.md`); first producer the requester's ad-sales agent (its baseline 2026-10-27 to 2026-11-10). Goal: interoperability | open: filed 2026-11-11 from board request `24b868d3`; the DEC proposed on a branch by 2026-11-14, accepted after its review rounds by 2026-11-21 | board request `24b868d3` (thread `b4c7840a`, 2026-11-10); the requester's proposal `docs/proposals/2026-11-10-agent-metrics-standard.md` at `464993dd`; CT-264; FAC-SEM-126 |

Fixed in the change that filed this row: the DEC-0151 linear-time check in `test/secret-shape.test.ts` (Windows, every night: the process's CPU clock moves in steps of about 15.6 ms, so `0 ms then 62 ms` and `15 ms then 125 ms` decided the ratio; a sample now repeats the read until 50 ms of CPU time and gives the time of one read) and the timeout of `names a slot by its place` in `test/source-rules.test.ts` (ubuntu, 5303 ms and 5324 ms against the 5000 ms default). Still owed: the proof, which only the scheduled run on ubuntu, macOS and Windows gives. | CT-290 | A setup step and its gates, one shape for every product that sets something up for a person (passioncode-platform PLAT-148, board message `0635ad44`, thread `638a2e88`, 2026-11-11; its report `docs/reports/2026-11-11-agent-first-setup/README.md` at `32a81ef`, sections 5.2 and 5.3). The launcher's doctor, the organization product's setup status, the host agent's receipts and the PM agent each report setup steps in their own form today, so Fabric Dashboards cannot show one ordered list without mapping each. Proposed: `setup-step/1` (`id`, `layer` computer, places, organization or people, `order`, `state` done, next, working, waiting or blocked, `actor` agent, person or machine, `gate`, `why`, `door`, `link`, `evidence`) and a closed vocabulary of eight human gates (sign-in, one-time-code, passkey, consent, attest, publish, payment, typed-secret; a spend above a limit is a consent). Owed: decide what is contract (the step record and the gate vocabulary) and what stays the product's; reuse AP-4's human steps and `human_steps` of the existing answers rather than a second list; schema, FAC-SEM codes, vectors; the reader rule (Dashboards renders the owners' records in order and computes nothing). | CT-292 | The context of a task, settled before an agent acts: an agent that can reach agents, accounts, keys, boards or data of more than one organization context (`personal` and each `org:<orgId>`, DEC-0082) settles which one a task is for from the sources the contract already has (the operator's words, the board request's Project, the folder's Project by DEC-0089, a grant's context, the place) and, when none settles it or they disagree, asks the operator once, explicitly, naming the candidate organizations and flows, never guessing and never acting in two for one task; the answer is recorded so no later agent asks again (the operator's word, 2026-11-12, this repository's session `7c9eb2c2`: when several organizations' agents are within reach and the project's context does not settle it, ask which flow or organization; "not a rule but in the skills, at the protocol level"). Owed: one DEC with `resolveTaskContext` and its vectors, an optional `context` in the execution context and the hand-over, a FAC-SEM code, and the adopters' changes (the adapter's skills, Fabric's session start and question surface, Switchboard's group organizations, Observatory's `project.resolve`). Goal: interoperability Read with DEC-0156 OW-3's no-person case (proposed on a branch, CT-287): OW-3 (its rounds 29 and 30) has a session with no person ask its waits through the organization's PM agent or the host's question surface, as TC-4 asks through the host's question surface and TC-6 keeps the candidates out of every board thread; the DEC makes them agree (the question as OW-3's named exception, its answer reaching a session that can act through TC-5's record, the board reply naming only an open step) and OW-3 cites it, whichever lands second (DEC-0156 round 28). Read also with DEC-0159 CF-3 (proposed on a branch, CT-218): a client key at a cloud instance in the local and cloud form carries no grant and no context, so TC-2's grant claim is absent for it; the DEC says which context a client-key call is in (the cloud instance's operator's, read as the place) and that TC-6 covers a client key as a credential; whichever lands second. | open: filed 2026-11-12; [the task context design](evidence/plans/2026-11-12-ct292-task-context.md) on main (TC-1 to TC-6); the DEC after its review rounds, target the organization slice of 2026-11-21 | the operator's word, 2026-11-12, session `7c9eb2c2`; [contexts](specification/contexts.md), DEC-0082, DEC-0089 |
| CT-293 | An order carries the access it needs: when the operator's word orders an act (send, publish, run) and doing it needs a grant, a scope, a key or a place on an allowlist, the agent's decision for that act also covers issuing a grant narrowed to exactly that act (that target, that scope, a short expiry) through the owner's issue door, with the operator's words as the decision and the issue recorded beside the act; a missing or unreadable credential is a step to fix, never a stop. Wider than the act (every chat, admin, another person's account, a new outside party), spend above a limit and the body's gates stay a decision of their own (AP-4, AP-5, MD-6). Today the contract has grants (DEC-0037 to DEC-0039), relayed decisions (AP-2, DEC-0121) and mandates (DEC-0125) but no rule that a decision for an act reaches the narrow grant the act needs, so every product answers it differently. Owed: decide whether AP-2's decision for an act covers its narrow grant, the narrowness test (target, scope, expiry) and the record; schema and vectors; read with DEC-0156 OW-1 and OW-4 and CT-250's levels. Goal: interoperability Read also with DEC-0156 OW-3's wait on an effect outside a grant and OW-5's kept limit (the narrow grant becomes their exception) and with DEC-0157 UD-3, UD-4 and UD-5 (both proposed on branches, CT-287): a decision for an act never covers turning back on a usage-data switch a person turned off, nor a grant whose scope reaches that switch, and a grant to read a product's raw usage events is a reader UD-3 binds and UD-5 names (DEC-0156 round 28, DEC-0157 round 25). Read also with DEC-0159 CF-3 and CF-4 (proposed on a branch, CT-218): a member's or another agent's client key is issued and rotated only by the operator in their own terminal or on the page, the key twin issues only its caller's key, no door widens a key's scopes beyond what its issuing could carry, and a key's scopes are changed by the operator's agent on the operator's word; the DEC restates them and says whether an act's decision covers the caller's own client key or a scope change; whichever lands second. | open: filed 2026-11-12 from the operator's standing rule of 2026-11-10 (an order includes the access it needs), surfaced by the review of which of the operator's rules belong in the protocol (the operator's word of 2026-11-12, session `7c9eb2c2`); design by 2026-11-21 | [service](specification/service.md) AP-2, AP-4; [delegation](specification/delegation.md) MD-6; DEC-0037 to DEC-0039 |
